乙型肝炎病毒相关肝细胞癌中TGF-β1/Smad2通路关键因子的表达及其与临床病理特征和预后的关系

Expression of key factors in the TGF-β1/Smad2 pathway and their associations with clinicopathological characteristics and prognosis in HBV-associated hepatocellular carcinoma
作者： Dan-Dan Sun, Zi-Long Wu
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 本研究探讨了TGF-β1和Smad2在乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)相关肝细胞癌(HCC)中的表达,并评估其与临床病理特征和预后的关系。方法 在这项前瞻性单中心研究中,于2022年4月至2024年4月期间纳入了120例乙型肝炎病毒相关肝细胞癌患者。通过免疫组化法检测配对肿瘤组织和相邻非肿瘤组织中TGF-β1和Smad2的表达。采用相关分析、逻辑回归、Kaplan-Meier生存分析和受试者工作特征(ROC)分析评估其与临床病理特征和预后的关系。随访时间为12–36个月。结果 肿瘤组织中TGF-β1和Smad2的表达显著高于配对的相邻非肿瘤组织(两者P < 0.001)。高表达与较差的分化程度和更晚期的分期相关(P < 0.05)。TGF-β1或Smad2表达阳性患者死亡率低于表达阴性者。预测死亡的AUC分别为TGF-β1为0.769,Smad2为0.756,联合模型为0.892。结论 乙型肝炎病毒相关肝细胞癌中肿瘤组织TGF-β1和Smad2的高表达与更具侵袭性的临床病理特征和较差的预后相关。与单独使用任一标志物相比,联合评估这两个标志物可能提高预后判别的能力;需要多中心验证。

本研究旨在探讨TGF-β1和Smad2在乙型肝炎病毒相关肝细胞癌中的表达,并评估其与临床病理特征和预后的关系。
设计与对象
进行了一项前瞻性单中心研究,于2022年4月至2024年4月期间纳入了120例乙型肝炎病毒相关肝细胞癌患者。

作者认为,肿瘤中TGF-β1和Smad2的高表达与更具侵袭性的临床病理特征和较差的预后相关,提示其可能作为疾病进展的生物标志物。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可能有助于开发靶向治疗,并改善乙型肝炎病毒相关肝细胞癌患者的预后评估。
局限与证据强度
该研究受限于单中心设计和相对较短的随访期,这可能影响其结果的普遍性。由于这些限制,证据强度中等。

Expression of key factors in the TGF-β1/Smad2 pathway and their associations with clinicopathological characteristics and prognosis in HBV-associated hepatocellular carcinoma

Authors: Dan-Dan Sun, Zi-Long Wu
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background This study investigated the expression of TGF-β1 and Smad2 in hepatitis B virus (HBV)-associated hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) and evaluated their associations with clinicopathological characteristics and prognosis. Methods In this prospective single-center cohort, 120 patients with HBV-associated HCC were enrolled between April 2022 and April 2024. TGF-β1 and Smad2 expression was assessed by immunohistochemistry in paired tumor and adjacent non-tumor tissues. Associations with clinicopathological characteristics and prognosis were examined using correlation analysis, logistic regression, Kaplan-Meier survival analysis, and receiver operating characteristic (ROC) analysis. Follow-up lasted 12–36 months. Results TGF-β1 and Smad2 expression was significantly higher in tumor tissue than in paired adjacent non-tumor tissue (both P < 0.001). Higher expression was associated with poorer differentiation and more advanced stage ( P < 0.05). Patients with positive TGF-β1 or Smad2 expression had lower survival rates than those with negative expression. The AUCs for predicting death were 0.769 for TGF-β1, 0.756 for Smad2, and 0.892 for the combined model. Conclusions Elevated tumor expression of TGF-β1 and Smad2 is associated with aggressive clinicopathological features and poorer prognosis in HBV-associated HCC. Combined assessment of the two markers may improve prognostic discrimination compared with either marker alone; multicenter validation is warranted.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to investigate the expression of TGF-β1 and Smad2 in HBV-associated hepatocellular carcinoma and evaluate their associations with clinicopathological characteristics and prognosis.
Design and population
A prospective single-center cohort study was conducted, enrolling 120 patients with HBV-associated hepatocellular carcinoma between April 2022 and April 2024.
Core methods
TGF-β1 and Smad2 expression was assessed by immunohistochemistry in paired tumor and adjacent non-tumor tissues. Associations with clinicopathological characteristics and prognosis were examined using correlation analysis, logistic regression, Kaplan-Meier survival analysis, and receiver operating characteristic (ROC) analysis.
Main results
TGF-β1 and Smad2 expression was significantly higher in tumor tissue than in paired adjacent non-tumor tissue (both P < 0.001). Higher expression was associated with poorer differentiation and more advanced stage (P < 0.05).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that elevated expression of TGF-β1 and Smad2 in tumors is linked to aggressive clinicopathological features and a poorer prognosis, suggesting their potential as biomarkers for disease progression.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings may inform the development of targeted therapies and improve prognostic assessment for patients with HBV-associated hepatocellular carcinoma.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its single-center design and relatively short follow-up period, which may affect generalizability. The evidence is moderate due to these limitations.

非洲孕妇中乙型肝炎病毒感染的相关因素:先前系统评价和荟萃分析的扩展

Associated factors of hepatitis B virus infection among pregnant women in Africa: an extension of a previous systematic review and meta-analysis on prevalence
作者： Yilma Markos Larebo, Zerfework Debebe Argago, Abebe Alemu Anshebo, Sujit Kumar Behera, Natarajan Gopalan
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素

我们使用纽卡斯尔-渥太华量表(NOS)评估了方法学质量。
证据基础与方法

共识与结论

Associated factors of hepatitis B virus infection among pregnant women in Africa: an extension of a previous systematic review and meta-analysis on prevalence

Authors: Yilma Markos Larebo, Zerfework Debebe Argago, Abebe Alemu Anshebo, Sujit Kumar Behera, Natarajan Gopalan
Original abstract (English)
Hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection is a significant public health concern among pregnant women in Africa. While previous reviews have focused on the prevalence of HBV, comprehensive evidence regarding the associated factors of HBV infection is limited. Therefore, this systematic review and meta-analysis aimed to identify the associated factors of HBV infection in pregnant women in Africa. We systematically searched PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, Advanced Google Scholar, the Cochrane Library, and African Journal Online for observational studies published between July 18, 2013, and July 18, 2023. Studies reporting adjusted effect estimates for factors associated with HBV infection among pregnant women were included. We assessed methodological quality using the Newcastle-Ottawa Scale (NOS). We conducted separate random-effects meta-analyses using the DerSimonian–Laird method for each associated factor reported by at least two studies to estimate pooled adjusted odds ratios (AORs) with 95% confidence intervals (CIs). Associated factors examined across multiple studies that differed substantially in exposure categories or reference groups were synthesized narratively. Statistical heterogeneity was assessed using the I2 statistic. Of the 19 included studies, 20 associated factors were eligible for the quantitative synthesis. Significant associations with HBV infection among pregnant women were a history of abortion (AOR = 3.08, 95% CI: 1.58–4.58), multiple sexual partners (AOR = 5.33, 95% CI: 2.50–8.17), blood transfusion (AOR = 2.64, 95% CI: 1.38–3.90), hospital admission (AOR = 3.28, 95% CI: 2.02–4.53), surgery (AOR = 2.48, 95% CI: 1.04–3.92), HIV infection (AOR = 9.22, 95% CI: 2.39–16.05), tattooing (AOR = 2.53, 95% CI: 1.26–3.80), female genital mutilation (AOR = 2.48, 95% CI: 2.02–2.94), dental procedures (AOR = 2.30, 95% CI: 1.16–3,44), family history of HBV infection (AOR = 3.37, 95% CI: 1.94–4.80), contact with jaundiced patients (AOR = 4.18, 95% CI: 2.09–6.27), tonsillectomy (AOR = 4.87, 95% CI: 3.36–6.37), and sharing sharp materials (AOR = 2.87, 95% CI: 2.47–3.27). This meta-analysis identified multiple behavioral, medical, and household-related associated factors of HBV infection. Strengthening routine antenatal HBV screening, targeted risk assessment, vaccination strategies, and linkage-to-care interventions may help improve early detection and reduce mother-to-child transmission of HBV. PROSPERO 2025 CRD420251240694.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
We assessed methodological quality using the Newcastle-Ottawa Scale (NOS).
Evidence base and method
We conducted separate random-effects meta-analyses using the DerSimonian–Laird method for each associated factor reported by at least two studies to estimate pooled adjusted odds ratios (AORs) with 95% confidence intervals (CIs).
Consensus and conclusions
Associated factors examined across multiple studies that differed substantially in exposure categories or reference groups were synthesized narratively.
Controversies and gaps
While previous reviews have focused on the prevalence of HBV, comprehensive evidence regarding the associated factors of HBV infection is limited.
Research and practice implications
Strengthening routine antenatal HBV screening, targeted risk assessment, vaccination strategies, and linkage-to-care interventions may help improve early detection and reduce mother-to-child transmission of HBV.

HIV携带者中乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)共感染在尼日利亚医学研究所拉各斯的肝相关发病率和死亡率:一项横断面研究

Hepatitis B Virus Knowledge, Preventive Practices, and Vaccination Status Among People Living with HIV at the Nigerian Institute of Medical Research, Lagos: A Cross-Sectional Study
作者： Musa Sanni Ladan, Akeem Blessing Lateef, Itohan Florence Ulikhifun, Motunrayo Mariam Oyelude
摘要中文翻译乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)共感染显著增加了HIV携带者(PLHIV)的肝相关发病率和死亡率。尽管疫苗接种仍然是预防HBV感染最有效的策略,但其普及率通常被认为取决于对疫苗接种的充分知识和积极态度。本研究评估了HIV阳性患者对HBV疫苗接种的知识、态度和行为,并探讨了知识是否显著预测疫苗接种的普及率。在尼日利亚医学研究所(NIMR)位于拉各斯州亚巴的机构中,对374名接受HIV治疗的患者进行了横断面调查。参与者通过目的抽样法选取,数据通过结构化问卷收集。数据使用SPSS 26.0版本进行分析,包括描述性统计、卡方检验和逻辑回归分析,统计学显著性设定为p<0.05。大多数受访者为女性(70.2%),已婚(62.9%),拥有高等教育背景(40.7%)。总体而言,82%的参与者对HBV感染和疫苗接种的知识水平较低(均值=1.20±0.443),而58%的受访者对HBV疫苗接种表现出负面态度(均值=2.73±0.580)。尽管如此,自我报告的HBV疫苗接种普及率相对较高(79.6%,均值=1.4\11±0.423)。医疗设施被确认为HBV相关信息的主要来源。教育水平与疫苗接种状态显著相关(p=0.025),而性别与知识水平显著相关(p=0.010)。职业与态度无显著相关性(p=0.466)。逻辑回归分析表明,知识不是疫苗接种普及率的显著预测因素(χ2=4.49,p=0.106,R2=0.018)。研究结果表明,知识与主要负面态度之间存在显著脱节。这挑战了传统假设,即知识必然驱动疫苗接种行为,并表明健康系统因素,包括常规抗病毒药物的提供和医疗保健提供者的建议,可能在HIV携带者中对疫苗接种普及率发挥更重要的作用。因此,干预措施应结合改善抗病毒药物的可及性、以医疗保健提供者为主导的疫苗接种策略以及有针对性的教育,特别是在将HBV疫苗接种纳入常规HIV护理中时。

乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)共感染显著增加了HIV携带者(PLHIV)的肝相关发病率和死亡率。

数据使用SPSS 26.0版本进行分析,包括描述性统计、卡方检验和逻辑回归分析,统计学显著性设定为p<0.05。
共识与结论
教育水平与疫苗接种状态显著相关(p=0.025),而性别与知识水平显著相关(p=0.010)。
争议与缺口
研究结果表明,知识与主要负面态度之间存在显著脱节。
科研与实践启示
这挑战了传统假设,即知识必然驱动疫苗接种行为,并表明健康系统因素,包括常规抗病毒药物的提供和医疗保健提供者的建议,可能在HIV携带者中对疫苗接种普及率发挥更重要的作用。

Hepatitis B Virus Knowledge, Preventive Practices, and Vaccination Status Among People Living with HIV at the Nigerian Institute of Medical Research, Lagos: A Cross-Sectional Study

Authors: Musa Sanni Ladan, Akeem Blessing Lateef, Itohan Florence Ulikhifun, Motunrayo Mariam Oyelude
Original abstract (English)
Hepatitis B Virus (HBV) co-infection substantially increases liver-related morbidity and mortality among people living with HIV (PLHIV). Although vaccination remains the most effective strategy for prevention HBV infection, its uptake is conventionally assumed to depend on adequate knowledge and positive attitudes towards vaccination. This study assessed knowledge, attitudes, and practices regarding HBV vaccination among HIV-positive patients and examined whether knowledge significantly predicts vaccination uptake. A cross-sectional institutional survey was conducted among 374 HIV-positive patients receiving care at the Nigerian Institute of Medical Research (NIMR), Yaba, Lagos State. Participants were selected using purposive sampling. and data were collected using a structured questionnaire. Data were analysed using SPSS version 26.0, through descriptive statistics, chi-square tests, and logistic regression with statistical significance set at p<0.05. The majority of respondents were female (70.2%), married (62.9%), and had tertiary-educated (40.7%). Overall, 82% of participants demonstrated low levels of knowledge regarding HBV infection and vaccination (Mean=1.20±0.443), while 58% exhibited negative attitudes toward HBV vaccination (Mean=2.73±0.580). Despite these findings, self-reported HBV vaccination uptake was relatively high (79.6%, Mean = 1.4\11±0.423). Healthcare facilities were identified as the primary source of HBV related information. Education level were significantly associated with vaccination status (p=0.025), while gender was significantly associated with knowledge level (p = 0.010). Occupation was not significantly associated with attitude (p = 0.466). Logistic regression analysis indicated that knowledge was not a significant predictor of vaccination uptake (χ2 = 4.49, p = 0.106, R2 = 0.018). The findings demonstrate a notable disconnect between knowledge and predominantly negative attitudes. This challenge the conventional assumption that knowledge necessarily drives vaccination behavior and suggest that health-system factors, including routine vaccine provision and health-care provider recommendations, may play a more important role in vaccination uptake among PLHIV. Interventions should therefore combine improved vaccine accessibility and provider-led vaccination strategies with targeted education, particularly through the integration of HBV vaccination into routine HIV care.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Hepatitis B Virus (HBV) co-infection substantially increases liver-related morbidity and mortality among people living with HIV (PLHIV).
Evidence base and method
Data were analysed using SPSS version 26.0, through descriptive statistics, chi-square tests, and logistic regression with statistical significance set at p<0.05.
Consensus and conclusions
Education level were significantly associated with vaccination status (p=0.025), while gender was significantly associated with knowledge level (p = 0.010).
Controversies and gaps
The findings demonstrate a notable disconnect between knowledge and predominantly negative attitudes.
Research and practice implications
This challenge the conventional assumption that knowledge necessarily drives vaccination behavior and suggest that health-system factors, including routine vaccine provision and health-care provider recommendations, may play a more important role in vaccination uptake among PLHIV.

资源匮乏地区乙型和丙型肝炎的护理点诊断:叙述性回顾

Point of Care Diagnostics for Hepatitis B and C in Low-resource Settings: A Narrative Review
作者： Abhay Saraf, Sunita Girish, Rohini Adepwar
摘要中文翻译乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 和丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 仍然是主要的公共卫生挑战,特别是在低收入和中等收入国家 (LMIC),实验室能力较差,早期发现疾病并有助于其管理的能力较差。正确、及时的诊断对于临床干预和全球消除至关重要。本叙述性综述探讨了使用即时护理 (POC) 检测来加强资源匮乏环境中 HBV 和 HCV 检测的潜力。审查了同行评审的文献、全球健康报告和灰色文献,以评估 POC 技术的最新技术、诊断性能、资源有限环境中的实施以及新的发展。还指出了改善及时有效的 HBV 和 HCV 诊断 的可及性的现有挑战和未来机遇。移动医疗集成、人工智能 (AI) 支持的解释和自我测试等新技术和功能增强了他们的能力。仍然存在一些挑战,包括政策采用不平衡、供应链问题和社会耻辱,导致难以扩展。POC 诊断有可能通过改善快速检测的机会来加速消除肝炎。它们的成功实施需要加强卫生系统、训练有素的医疗保健人员以及融入现有的医疗保健服务。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点探讨了在资源有限的环境中,快速诊断(Point-Of-Care,POC)技术在改善乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)感染的检测和管理中的作用。核心问题是:POC检测是否能够提高HBV诊断的可及性和有效性,特别是在实验室基础设施有限的地区。
证据基础与方法
本综述是对同行评审文献、全球健康报告和灰色文献的叙述性综合分析。它评估了POC技术的状态、诊断性能以及在资源有限环境中实施的挑战。综述未报告具体的纳入标准、研究数量或元分析方法。
共识与结论
POC诊断技术有潜力通过提高快速检测的可及性来加速肝炎的消除。其成功实施需要加强卫生系统、培训卫生工作者,并将其整合到现有的卫生服务中。新技术,如移动医疗整合和人工智能辅助解读,可以增强其能力。
争议与缺口
仍然存在一些挑战领域,包括政策采纳不均、供应链问题和社会污名化,这些因素使得在HBV中扩大POC诊断技术的应用变得困难。综述指出,现有挑战和未来改善HBV诊断及时性和有效性的机会仍未得到充分探索。
科研与实践启示
提高HBV诊断的及时性和有效性对于临床干预和全球消除肝炎至关重要。综述强调需要解决供应链问题、政策采纳和社会污名化,以扩大POC诊断技术的应用。同时,它还建议将新技术整合到卫生系统中,以增强诊断能力。

Point of Care Diagnostics for Hepatitis B and C in Low-resource Settings: A Narrative Review

Authors: Abhay Saraf, Sunita Girish, Rohini Adepwar
Original abstract (English)
Hepatitis B Virus (HBV) and Hepatitis C Virus (HCV) continue to be major public health challenge especially in Low- and MiddleIncome Countries (LMICs), with poor laboratory capacity and the ability to detect the disease earlier and contribute to its management. Proper and timely diagnosis is crucial for clinical intervention and global elimination. The present narrative review explores the potential for the use of Point-Of-Care (POC) testing to enhance the testing of HBV and HCV in low-resource settings. Peer-reviewed literature, global health reports, and grey literature were reviewed to assess the state of the art of POC technologies, diagnostic performance, and implementation in resource-limited settings, as well as new developments. Existing challenges and future opportunities for improving access to timely and effective HBV and HCV diagnosis are also pointed out. New technologies and features, such as mobile healthcare integration, Artificial Intelligence (AI)-powered interpretation, and self-testing, enhance their capabilities. There are still areas of challenge, including uneven policy adoption, supply chain issues, and social stigma, that make it difficult to scale. POC diagnostics have the potential to accelerate hepatitis elimination by improving access to rapid testing. Their successful implementation requires strengthened health systems, trained healthcare workers, and integration into existing healthcare services.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the role of Point-Of-Care (POC) diagnostics in improving the detection and management of Hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection in low-resource settings. The central question is whether POC testing can enhance the accessibility and effectiveness of HBV diagnosis, particularly in regions with limited laboratory infrastructure.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of peer-reviewed literature, global health reports, and grey literature. It assesses the state of POC technologies, their diagnostic performance, and implementation challenges in resource-limited settings. The review does not report specific eligibility criteria, study counts, or meta-analysis methods.
Consensus and conclusions
POC diagnostics have the potential to accelerate hepatitis elimination by improving access to rapid testing. Their successful implementation requires strengthened health systems, trained healthcare workers, and integration into existing healthcare services. New technologies, such as mobile healthcare integration and AI-powered interpretation, enhance their capabilities.
Controversies and gaps
There are still areas of challenge, including uneven policy adoption, supply chain issues, and social stigma, that make it difficult to scale POC diagnostics for HBV. The review highlights that existing challenges and future opportunities for improving access to timely and effective diagnosis remain underexplored.
Research and practice implications
Improving access to timely and effective HBV diagnosis is crucial for clinical intervention and global elimination. The review emphasizes the need for addressing supply chain issues, policy adoption, and social stigma to scale POC diagnostics. It also suggests the importance of integrating new technologies into healthcare systems to enhance diagnostic capabilities.

在尼日利亚北中部部分州通过广播信息减少公众对乙型肝炎疫苗接种犹豫态度的效果研究

Effectiveness of Radio Messages in Reducing Public Hesitancy Toward Hepatitis B Vaccination in Selected North-Central States of Nigeria
作者： Abu Adamu1
摘要中文翻译乙型肝炎仍然是尼日利亚的主要公共卫生挑战,低疫苗接种接种率因广泛存在的公众犹豫态度而加剧,这种犹豫态度受到错误信息、文化误解以及难以获得可靠健康信息的影响,尤其是在北中部各州。应对这一挑战需要有效的健康沟通策略,以提高公众意识、信任度和疫苗接种意愿。本研究探讨了广播信息在减少公众对乙型肝炎疫苗接种犹豫态度方面的有效性,依据计划行为理论(Theory of Planned Behavior),该理论因其强调态度、主观规范和感知行为控制如何影响意图和健康相关决策而被采用。研究采用调查研究设计,通过结构化问卷收集了选定州居民的数据,并辅以从广播节目档案和公共卫生报告中获取的次级数据。问卷回答通过简单百分比分析来量化公众在认知、知识和行为方面的趋势,次级数据则通过定性内容分析来评估信息框架、文化相关性和说服策略。研究结果表明,广播信息的有效性受到信息设计不当、文化不匹配和说服内容薄弱等因素的严重影响,这些因素限制了知识获取、信任建立和恐惧减少,从而维持了疫苗的犹豫态度。此外,广播作为健康沟通工具的潜力受到基础设施限制、受众多样性、语言障碍、不足的节目策略以及缺乏社区参与的制约,这些因素降低了信息的传播范围、相关性和对疫苗接种决策的影响。研究建议应仔细评估并重新设计广播信息,以确保其文化相关性、清晰度和具有说服力的内容,从而建立信任并减少恐惧。同时,广播电台和卫生部门应通过扩大覆盖范围至偏远地区、采用多种当地语言以及采用互动访谈节目和证言等吸引人的节目策略,来解决基础设施和受众相关的问题,以提高公众对乙型肝炎疫苗接种的接受度和接种率。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究聚焦于在尼日利亚北中部部分州通过广播信息减少公众对乙型肝炎疫苗接种犹豫态度的效果。核心问题是广播信息是否能够提高公众对乙型肝炎疫苗接种的意识、信任度和疫苗接种意愿,特别是在错误信息、文化误解和难以获得可靠健康信息的背景下。
证据基础与方法
本研究采用调查研究设计,通过向选定州的居民发放结构化问卷收集数据。次级数据来源于广播节目档案和公共卫生报告。问卷回答通过简单百分比分析来量化公众在认知、知识和行为方面的趋势,次级数据则通过定性内容分析来评估信息框架、文化相关性和说服策略。
共识与结论
研究发现,由于信息设计不佳、文化不匹配和说服内容薄弱,广播信息在减少公众对乙型肝炎疫苗接种的犹豫态度方面效果有限。这些因素限制了知识获取、信任建立和恐惧减少,从而维持了疫苗的犹豫态度。研究结果表明,在尼日利亚北中部各州,广播作为健康沟通工具在乙型肝炎疫苗接种相关情境下存在显著局限性。
争议与缺口
本研究指出了广播信息在应对公众犹豫态度方面的局限性,但未提供与其他沟通策略的比较数据。此外,未提及广播信息在不同人口群体中的效果差异,以及社会经济状况或教育水平等因素如何影响疫苗接种意愿。证据还缺乏关于广播信息对疫苗接种行为的长期影响的信息。
科研与实践启示
本研究建议应仔细评估并重新设计广播信息,以确保其文化相关性、清晰度和具有说服力的内容,从而建立信任并减少恐惧。同时,广播电台和卫生部门应通过扩大覆盖范围至偏远地区、采用多种当地语言以及采用互动访谈节目和证言等吸引人的节目策略,来解决基础设施和受众相关的问题,以提高公众对乙型肝炎疫苗接种的接受度和接种率。

Effectiveness of Radio Messages in Reducing Public Hesitancy Toward Hepatitis B Vaccination in Selected North-Central States of Nigeria

Authors: Abu Adamu1
Original abstract (English)
Hepatitis B remains a major public health challenge in Nigeria, with low vaccination uptake exacerbated by widespread public hesitancy fueled by misinformation, cultural misconceptions, and limited access to reliable health information, particularly in the North-Central states. Addressing this challenge necessitates effective health communication strategies capable of improving awareness, trust, and vaccine acceptance. This study examined the effectiveness of radio messages in reducing public hesitancy toward Hepatitis B vaccination, guided by the Theory of Planned Behavior, which was adopted due to its emphasis on understanding how attitudes, subjective norms, and perceived behavioral control influence intention and health-related decision-making. Employing a survey research design, data were collected through structured questionnaires administered to residents in selected states, complemented by secondary data obtained from radio program archives and public health reports. Questionnaire responses were analyzed using simple percentages to quantify trends in perception, knowledge, and behavior, while secondary data underwent qualitative content analysis to assess message framing, cultural relevance, and persuasive strategies. Findings revealed that the effectiveness of radio messages was significantly undermined by ineffective message design, cultural misalignment, and weak persuasive content, limiting knowledge acquisition, trust building, and fear reduction, thereby sustaining vaccine hesitancy. Additionally, radio’s potential as a health communication tool was constrained by infrastructural limitations, diverse audience characteristics, linguistic barriers, inadequate programming strategies, and insufficient community engagement, all of which diminished message reach, relevance, and influence on vaccination decisions. The study recommends that radio messages be carefully evaluated and redesigned to ensure cultural relevance, clarity, and persuasive content that builds trust and reduces fear, and that radio stations and health authorities address infrastructural and audience-related challenges by expanding coverage to remote areas, incorporating multiple local languages, and adopting engaging programming strategies such as interactive talk shows and testimonials to enhance public receptivity and uptake of Hepatitis B vaccination.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on the effectiveness of radio messages in reducing public hesitancy toward Hepatitis B vaccination in selected North-Central states of Nigeria. The central question is whether radio messages can improve awareness, trust, and vaccine acceptance among the population, particularly in the context of misinformation, cultural misconceptions, and limited access to reliable health information.
Evidence base and method
The review employs a survey research design, collecting data through structured questionnaires administered to residents in selected states. Secondary data were obtained from radio program archives and public health reports. Questionnaire responses were analyzed using simple percentages to quantify trends in perception, knowledge, and behavior, while secondary data underwent qualitative content analysis to assess message framing, cultural relevance, and persuasive strategies.
Consensus and conclusions
The study found that radio messages were ineffective in reducing public hesitancy toward Hepatitis B vaccination due to poor message design, cultural misalignment, and weak persuasive content. These factors limited knowledge acquisition, trust building, and fear reduction, thereby sustaining vaccine hesitancy. The findings suggest that radio as a health communication tool has significant limitations in the context of Hepatitis B vaccination in the North-Central states of Nigeria.
Controversies and gaps
The study highlights the limitations of radio messages in addressing public hesitancy, but it does not provide comparative data with other communication strategies. There is no mention of how the effectiveness of radio messages might vary across different demographic groups or how other factors such as socioeconomic status or education level might influence vaccine acceptance. The evidence also lacks information on the long-term impact of radio messages on vaccination behavior.
Research and practice implications
The study recommends that radio messages be carefully evaluated and redesigned to ensure cultural relevance, clarity, and persuasive content that builds trust and reduces fear. It also suggests that radio stations and health authorities should address infrastructural and audience-related challenges by expanding coverage to remote areas, incorporating multiple local languages, and adopting engaging programming strategies such as interactive talk shows and testimonials to enhance public receptivity and uptake of Hepatitis B vaccination.

慢性乙型肝炎病毒血清学清除:巴西东北部大学医院的经验

SEROLOGICAL CLEARANCE OF HBSAG IN CHRONIC HEPATITIS B: EXPERIENCE IN A UNIVERSITY HOSPITAL
作者： A. Amorim, Alessandra Porto de Macedo Costa
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景:慢性乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)感染由于其高发病率以及与肝硬化和肝细胞癌相关的特性,构成了重大的公共卫生问题。功能性治愈,以HBV表面抗原(HBsAg)的血清学清除为特征,是主要的治疗目标,因为目前尚无法实现病毒的完全清除。HBsAg清除率在文献中存在较大差异,了解与这一事件相关的因素对于优化患者管理至关重要。目的:探讨慢性乙型肝炎病毒感染患者在巴西东北部参考中心随访的队列研究中HBsAg清除率,并识别与该事件相关的流行病学、临床和病毒学因素。方法:通过回顾2015年1月至2025年10月期间在巴西东北部某肝病科治疗的慢性乙型肝炎病毒感染患者的医疗记录,进行了一项回顾性队列研究研究。分析了临床、人口统计学和实验室变量。统计分析采用Mann-Whitney检验用于定量变量,Fisher精确检验用于分类变量,同时构建Kaplan-Meier曲线并使用Log-Rank检验评估HBsAg清除情况。显著性水平设定为P<0.05。结果:在筛选的600份医疗记录中,有299名患者被纳入分析,其中63.2%为女性,中位年龄为45岁。在平均随访期5.14年,总计1,538人年的观察中,12名患者(4%)实现了HBsAg的血清学清除,年清除率为0.78%。统计分析显示,HBsAg清除与年龄较大(P=0.008)和较低的HBV-DNA水平(P=0.0156)存在显著关联。结论:在该队列研究中,HBsAg血清学清除的年发生率为0.78%。年龄较大和病毒载量较低与功能性治愈显著相关。这些发现丰富了该地区慢性乙型肝炎病毒自然史的知识,并重申了病毒抑制在疾病控制中的重要性。

证据基础与方法
通过回顾2015年1月至2025年10月期间在巴西东北部某肝病科治疗的慢性乙型肝炎病毒感染患者的医疗记录,进行了一项回顾性队列研究研究。
共识与结论
年龄较大和病毒载量较低与功能性治愈显著相关。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据没有明确指出争议或证据差距。

这些发现丰富了该地区慢性乙型肝炎病毒自然史的知识,并重申了病毒抑制在疾病控制中的重要性。

SEROLOGICAL CLEARANCE OF HBSAG IN CHRONIC HEPATITIS B: EXPERIENCE IN A UNIVERSITY HOSPITAL

Authors: A. Amorim, Alessandra Porto de Macedo Costa
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background: Chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection represents a major public health problem due to its high morbidity and mortality associated with liver cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma. Functional cure, characterized by serological clearance of the HBV surface antigen (HBsAg), is the main therapeutic goal, since sterilizing viral eradication is not yet feasible. HBsAg clearance rates vary widely in the literature, and understanding the factors associated with this event is crucial for optimizing patient management. Objective: To investigate the HBsAg clearance rate and identify the demographic, clinical, and virological factors associated with this event in a cohort of patients with chronic hepatitis B virus infection followed up at a reference center in Northeast Brazil. Methods: A retrospective cohort study was conducted through a review of the medical records of patients with chronic hepatitis B virus infection treated at a Hepatology Service in Northeast Brazil between January 2015 and October 2025. Clinical, demographic and laboratory variables were analyzed. Statistical analysis used tests such as Mann-Whitney for quantitative variables and Fisher’s exact test for categorical variables, in addition to the construction of Kaplan-Meier curves with the Log-Rank test to evaluate HBsAg clearance. The significance level was set at P<0.05. Results: Of the 600 medical records screened, 299 patients were included in the analysis, 63.2% of whom were women, with a median age of 45 years. During a mean follow-up period of 5.14 years, totaling 1,538 person-years, 12 patients (4%) achieved serological clearance of HBsAg, resulting in an annual rate of 0.78%. Statistical analysis revealed significant associations between HBsAg clearance and advanced age (P=0.008) and lower HBV-DNA levels (P=0.0156). Conclusion: The annual rate of HBsAg serological clearance in this cohort was 0.78%. Advanced age and low viral load levels were significantly associated with functional cure. These findings enrich the knowledge about the natural history of chronic hepatitis B in the region and reaffirm the importance of viral suppression in disease control.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
To investigate the HBsAg clearance rate and identify the demographic, clinical, and virological factors associated with this event in a cohort of patients with chronic hepatitis B virus infection followed up at a reference center in Northeast Brazil.
Evidence base and method
A retrospective cohort study was conducted through a review of the medical records of patients with chronic hepatitis B virus infection treated at a Hepatology Service in Northeast Brazil between January 2015 and October 2025.
Consensus and conclusions
Advanced age and low viral load levels were significantly associated with functional cure.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not clearly state controversies or evidence gaps.
Research and practice implications
These findings enrich the knowledge about the natural history of chronic hepatitis B in the region and reaffirm the importance of viral suppression in disease control.

功能性治愈研究中核心障碍及免疫机制

The core obstacles and immunological mechanisms in the study of functional cure for Chronic Hepatitis B
作者： Lesheng Lin, Dongshan Yu, Shuilin Sun
摘要中文翻译实现功能性治愈是慢性乙型肝炎(CHB)管理中的首要治疗目标,因为它显著降低了肝细胞癌的发病率并减缓进展至终末期肝病的过程。尽管如此,目前CHB患者中实现功能性治愈的比例仍低于30%。实现这一目标的主要障碍包括病毒共价闭合环状DNA(共价闭合环状DNA)的持续存在、病毒基因组整合至宿主基因组中、由于长期暴露于高水平病毒抗原导致的免疫耐受,以及宿主介导的HBV特异性免疫耗竭和在多个调控层面的功能障碍。对这些障碍背后的分子通路及其相关免疫调控网络进行深入阐明,对于开发新的治疗策略至关重要。本综述全面整合了近期关于HBV持续存在的病毒学机制、宿主对HBV感染的免疫应答以及新兴免疫治疗干预措施的研究进展。其目的是建立理论基础并提供关键视角,以支持旨在实现功能性治愈的CHB治疗方案的临床转化。

争议与缺口
对这些障碍背后的分子通路及其相关免疫调控网络进行深入阐明,对于开发新的治疗策略至关重要。
科研与实践启示

The core obstacles and immunological mechanisms in the study of functional cure for Chronic Hepatitis B

Authors: Lesheng Lin, Dongshan Yu, Shuilin Sun
Original abstract (English)
Attaining a functional cure constitutes the paramount therapeutic objective in the management of chronic hepatitis B (CHB), as it markedly reduces the incidence of hepatocellular carcinoma and the progression to terminal liver disease. Despite this, the current functional cure rate among CHB patients remains below 30%. Major impediments to achieving this outcome include the persistent existence of viral covalently closed circular DNA (cccDNA), integration of the viral genome into the host’s DNA, immune tolerance resulting from prolonged exposure to elevated viral antigen levels, and host-mediated HBV-specific immune exhaustion and dysfunction at multiple regulatory levels. A thorough elucidation of the molecular pathways underpinning these barriers, alongside their associated immune regulatory networks, is critical for the advancement of novel therapeutic strategies. This review comprehensively integrates recent progress in understanding the virological mechanisms responsible for HBV persistence, host immune responses to HBV infection, and emerging immunotherapeutic interventions. The aim is to establish a theoretical foundation and provide critical perspectives to support the clinical translation of approaches designed to achieve a functional cure for CHB.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Attaining a functional cure constitutes the paramount therapeutic objective in the management of chronic hepatitis B (CHB), as it markedly reduces the incidence of hepatocellular carcinoma and the progression to terminal liver disease.
Evidence base and method
This review comprehensively integrates recent progress in understanding the virological mechanisms responsible for HBV persistence, host immune responses to HBV infection, and emerging immunotherapeutic interventions.
Consensus and conclusions
Major impediments to achieving this outcome include the persistent existence of viral covalently closed circular DNA (cccDNA), integration of the viral genome into the host’s DNA, immune tolerance resulting from prolonged exposure to elevated viral antigen levels, and host-mediated HBV-specific immune exhaustion and dysfunction at multiple regulatory levels.
Controversies and gaps
A thorough elucidation of the molecular pathways underpinning these barriers, alongside their associated immune regulatory networks, is critical for the advancement of novel therapeutic strategies.
Research and practice implications
The aim is to establish a theoretical foundation and provide critical perspectives to support the clinical translation of approaches designed to achieve a functional cure for CHB.

基线乙型肝炎表面抗原和肝硬化在确定干扰素 治疗 持续时间方面的生物学解释

Biological interpretation of baseline hepatitis B surface antigen and cirrhosis in determining interferon treatment duration
作者： Ling-Feng Zou, Shao-Ding Yu, Yan Luo, Cheng-Long Wang
摘要中文翻译最近将基线乙型肝炎表面抗原和肝硬化确定为延长干扰素治疗的独立预测因子,提供了有价值的临床框架,但这些关联背后的生物学机制需要进一步阐明。在本意见综述中,我们不仅将这些因素解释为统计协变量,而且将其解释为病毒-宿主 平衡的病理替代物。我们认为,高基线乙型肝炎表面抗原可能反映了肝内根深蒂固的病毒 储存宿主,并可能创造一个富含抗原的环境,损害有效的 抗病毒药物 免疫力。同时,肝硬化可以更好地理解为一系列结构重塑,其中血管变化和肝窦开窗可能限制免疫 监测 并影响局部治疗途径。因此,可能需要(至少部分)超过 48 周的 治疗 持续时间来克服这些深刻的免疫学和结构障碍。我们主张将未来的预测模型从二元临床定义转变为病毒动力学和组织学形态的精细整合,以完善 慢性乙型肝炎 中的精准医学。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
最近发现基线乙型肝炎表面抗原和肝硬化是延长干扰素治疗的独立预测因子,为临床提供了有价值的框架,但这些关联背后的生物学机制仍需进一步阐明。
证据基础与方法
在本观点综述中,我们认为这些因素不仅仅是统计学上的协变量,而是病毒与宿主平衡的病理替代标志。
共识与结论
我们提出,较高的基线乙型肝炎表面抗原水平可能反映了乙型肝炎病毒在肝内高度固着的储存宿主,并可能形成抗原丰富的环境,从而损害有效的抗病毒药物免疫应答。
争议与缺口
同时,肝硬化可能更好地被理解为一种结构重塑的谱系,在其中血管变化和窦状隙的破坏可能限制免疫监测,并影响局部治疗的可及性。
科研与实践启示
因此,可能需要(至少部分)超过 48 周的 治疗 持续时间来克服这些深刻的免疫学和结构障碍。

Biological interpretation of baseline hepatitis B surface antigen and cirrhosis in determining interferon treatment duration

Authors: Ling-Feng Zou, Shao-Ding Yu, Yan Luo, Cheng-Long Wang
Original abstract (English)
The recent identification of baseline hepatitis B surface antigen and cirrhosis as independent predictors of extended interferon therapy provides a valuable clinical framework, yet the biological mechanisms underlying these associations require further elucidation. In this opinion review, we interpret these factors not merely as statistical covariates but as pathological surrogates for the virus-host equilibrium. We propose that high baseline hepatitis B surface antigen may reflect a deeply entrenched intrahepatic viral reservoir and may create an antigen-rich environment that impairs effective antiviral immunity. Concurrently, cirrhosis may be better understood as a spectrum of architectural remodeling in which vascular changes and sinusoidal defenestration could limit immune surveillance and affect local therapeutic access. Consequently, treatment durations exceeding 48 weeks may be required, at least in part, to overcome these profound immunological and structural barriers. We advocate shifting future predictive modeling from binary clinical definitions to a granular integration of viral kinetics and histological morphology to refine precision medicine in chronic hepatitis B.

Consensus and conclusions

Controversies and gaps
Concurrently, cirrhosis may be better understood as a spectrum of architectural remodeling in which vascular changes and sinusoidal defenestration could limit immune surveillance and affect local therapeutic access.
Research and practice implications
Consequently, treatment durations exceeding 48 weeks may be required, at least in part, to overcome these profound immunological and structural barriers.

欧洲预防与护理标准:艾滋病、乙型肝炎和梅毒产前筛查模块

European standards of prevention and care: Module on antenatal screening for HIV, hepatitis B and syphilis
新闻简报The European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) and the European AIDS Clinical Society (EACS) have developed standards of care for antenatal screening of HIV, hepatitis B, and syphilis. These standards aim to prevent vertical transmission and ensure equitable, high-quality care for pregnant and breastfeeding individuals. In 2024, 196 newly diagnosed HIV infections in the EU/EEA were reported as resulting from vertical transmission, with 75% among migrants. There were 140 confirmed congenital syphilis cases in 28 EU/EEA countries, corresponding to a notification rate of 4.7 per 100,000 live births. For hepatitis B, only 1% of acute cases in 2023 were due to vertical transmission, and 39% of chronic cases had vertical transmission as the most common route, though 91% were classified as imported. The report highlights the importance of comprehensive data collection and the need for improved reporting mechanisms to better understand and address vertical transmission risks. The standards are based on scientific rationale, best practices, and ethical considerations, with a focus on accessibility and non-discrimination.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于2026-09-24发布。有关HIV和梅毒的垂直传播数据可供参考,文件讨论了2023和2024期间的情况。对于乙型肝炎,引用了2023年的数据。
地点与对象
欧洲联盟/欧洲经济区(EU/EEA)是报告的地点。人口包括怀孕和哺乳期的个体,特别是那些面临垂直传播风险的人群,同时提供了关于活产和慢性/急性乙型肝炎病例的数据。
事件
事件是制定欧洲产前筛查艾滋病、乙型肝炎和梅毒的标准,包括垂直传播率的数据。该报告由欧洲疾病预防控制中心(ECDC)和欧洲艾滋病临床学会(EACS)发布。
规模、影响与风险
In 2024, 196 newly diagnosed HIV infections in the EU/EEA were reported as resulting from vertical transmission, with 75% among migrants. For congenital syphilis, there were 140 confirmed cases in 28 EU/EEA countries. For hepatitis B, 1% of acute cases were due to vertical transmission, and 39% of chronic cases had vertical transmission as the most common route, though 91% were classified as imported.
应对、状态与不确定性
该报告概述了针对艾滋病、乙型肝炎和梅毒的护理标准,包括质量声明和结果指标。其基于科学依据和现行指南。关于垂直传播的数据已被报告,但指出乙型肝炎数据的完整性有限。
A high priorityPublic-health news

European standards of prevention and care: Module on antenatal screening for HIV, hepatitis B and syphilis

News Brief
The European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) and the European AIDS Clinical Society (EACS) have developed standards of care for antenatal screening of HIV, hepatitis B, and syphilis. These standards aim to prevent vertical transmission and ensure equitable, high-quality care for pregnant and breastfeeding individuals. In 2024, 196 newly diagnosed HIV infections in the EU/EEA were reported as resulting from vertical transmission, with 75% among migrants. There were 140 confirmed congenital syphilis cases in 28 EU/EEA countries, corresponding to a notification rate of 4.7 per 100,000 live births. For hepatitis B, only 1% of acute cases in 2023 were due to vertical transmission, and 39% of chronic cases had vertical transmission as the most common route, though 91% were classified as imported. The report highlights the importance of comprehensive data collection and the need for improved reporting mechanisms to better understand and address vertical transmission risks. The standards are based on scientific rationale, best practices, and ethical considerations, with a focus on accessibility and non-discrimination.

strengthening collaboration, preparedness & response Home Publications and data European standards of prevention and care: Module on antenatal screening for HIV, hepatitis B and syphilis Publications and data European standards of prevention and care: Module on antenatal screening for HIV, hepatitis B and syphilis Operational support 24 September 2026 The global community has committed to eliminating vertical transmission of HIV, hepatitis B virus (HBV) and syphilis as a public health priority. This global commitment encourages countries to provide the most effective, high-quality and person-centred care available to pregnant and breastfeeding people, aiming to ensure a generation born free of HIV, syphilis and HBV. Antenatal screening (ANS) programmes, including universal screening for transmissible infections, aim to ensure optimal parent and child health for all including population groups identified as vulnerable to vertical transmission in line with the overarching objective of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) of leaving no one behind. In the European Union/European Economic Area (EU/EEA), the number of births was 3.67 million in 2023. Data on the annual number of people living with HIV giving birth are not available at European level. However, data on vertical transmission of HIV and syphilis are available. In 2024, 196 newly diagnosed HIV infections in the EU/EEA were reported as resulting from vertical transmission. Of these, 75% (147 cases) were among migrants, defined as people whose country of origin was outside the reporting country. In 2024, there were 140 reports of confirmed congenital syphilis in 28 reporting EU/EEA countries, corresponding to an overall EU/EEA notification rate of 4.7 per 100 000 live births. Of the 63 cases with available data, the maternal country of birth differed from the reporting country in 17 (27%) cases. From 2023 to 2024, there was nearly a doubling of congenital syphilis cases reported by EU/EEA countries. For hepatitis B, data on transmission were complete for 24% of the acute hepatitis B cases reported in 2023 in the EU/EEA countries, of which only 1% were due to vertical transmission. Data on transmission was complete for 9% of chronic hepatitis B cases. For the 1 421 newly diagnosed chronic cases with complete information, vertical transmission was the most common route of transmission (39%) but the majority (91%) were classified as being imported meaning vertical transmission occurred in countries of origin and not in the reporting country. Publication file European Standards of prevention and care: Module on Antenatal Screening for HIV, hepatitis B and syphilis English (1.44 MB - PDF) Download What are standards of care for HIV? The standards of care (SoC) for HIV define the expected, or desired, quality of prevention, treatment, and care forpeople at risk of HIV acquisition or living with HIV. The standards are based on a scientific rationale, as well as the responsibilities of each stakeholder and ensure that people receive appropriate, high-quality prevention and care that aligns with the most up-to-date medical knowledge and ethical standards. The European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) in partnership with the European AIDS Clinical Society (EACS) have developed standards of care in the areas of HIV testing, pre-exposure prophylaxis (PrEP), antenatal screening, commencement of ART, and HIV and co-morbidities (add links to SoC modules). Each standard is based on the following structure: 1. Brief description of the rationale for the standard. 2. Quality statements describing best practice based on current guidelines, evidence, and expert opinion. 3. related measurable and auditable outcome indicators used to assess the quality and effectiveness of the services. 4. Numeric values for defined targets. The standards are person-centred in their approach with a specific focus on being equitable, non-discriminatory, relevant, appropriate, and accessible for people at risk of or living with HIV. More on this topic HIV infection and AIDS ECDC/EACS standards of HIV care Sustainable Development Goals
View five news elements
Time
The report was published on 2026-09-24. Data on vertical transmission of HIV and syphilis are available, and the document discusses the 2023 and 2024 periods. For hepatitis B, data from 2023 are referenced.
Location and population
The European Union/European Economic Area (EU/EEA) is the location. The population includes pregnant and breastfeeding individuals, particularly those at risk of vertical transmission, and data on live births and chronic/acute hepatitis B cases are provided.
Event
The event is the development of European standards for antenatal screening for HIV, hepatitis B, and syphilis, including data on vertical transmission rates. The report is published by the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) and the European AIDS Clinical Society (EACS).
Scale, impact and risk
In 2024, 196 newly diagnosed HIV infections in the EU/EEA were reported as resulting from vertical transmission, with 75% among migrants. For congenital syphilis, there were 140 confirmed cases in 28 EU/EEA countries. For hepatitis B, 1% of acute cases were due to vertical transmission, and 39% of chronic cases had vertical transmission as the most common route, though 91% were classified as imported.
Response, status and uncertainty
The report outlines the development of standards of care for HIV, hepatitis B, and syphilis, including quality statements and outcome indicators. It is based on scientific rationale and current guidelines. The data on vertical transmission are reported, but the completeness of data for hepatitis B is noted as limited.

印度承诺到2030年消除乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎作为公共卫生威胁,卫生部长称 - 新闻快报

India committed to eliminating Hepatitis B, C as public health threats by 2030, says Health Secretary - News On AIR
新闻简报印度重申了到2030年消除乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎作为公共卫生威胁的承诺,正如联合卫生部长Punya Salila Srivastava在纽约联合国大会第81届会议期间的一次边会中所强调的。该承诺包括在整个预防、筛查、诊断、治疗和治疗过程中加强干预措施,例如产前筛查、乙型肝炎疫苗接种、免费诊断和治疗、安全的输血服务以及对注射毒品者的咨询和康复支持。卫生部长还强调了全球合作、预防、检测和治疗的公平获取以及更强健的卫生系统对于实现这些目标的重要性。虽然没有报告具体的病例、死亡或暴露数量,但应对工作正在进行中,并涉及全面的策略。该承诺反映了长期的公共卫生倡议,而不是立即的暴发或事件。

A high priorityPublic-health news

India committed to eliminating Hepatitis B, C as public health threats by 2030, says Health Secretary - News On AIR

News Brief
India has reaffirmed its commitment to eliminating Hepatitis B and C as public health threats by 2030, as stated by Union Health Secretary Punya Salila Srivastava during a side event of the 81st session of the UN General Assembly in New York. The commitment includes strengthening interventions across the continuum of prevention, screening, diagnosis, treatment, and care, such as antenatal screening, Hepatitis B immunisation, free diagnosis and treatment, safe blood transfusion services, and support for people who inject drugs. The Health Secretary also stressed the importance of global cooperation, equitable access to prevention, testing, and treatment, and stronger health systems to achieve these targets. While no specific case, death, or exposure counts were reported, the response is ongoing and involves a comprehensive strategy. The commitment reflects a long-term public health initiative rather than an immediate outbreak or incident.

$3 million NHMRC CRE awarded to accelerate hepatitis B and HTLV-1 cure research - Doherty Institute

$3 million NHMRC CRE awarded to accelerate hepatitis B and HTLV-1 cure research - Doherty Institute
新闻简报The Doherty Institute, a research organization based in Australia, has been awarded a $3 million NHMRC CRE grant to accelerate the development of cures for hepatitis B and HTLV-1. This funding is part of a broader initiative to improve health outcomes through research, public health, and education. The grant announcement was published on 17th September, 2026, and it highlights the Institute's focus on infectious disease prevention, treatment, and cure. While the grant aims to advance research on these viruses, no specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers are reported in the evidence. The response status is under investigation, as the outcomes of the research are not yet available. The source is an official statement from the Doherty Institute, and there are no limitations in the content provided.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

乙型肝炎病毒基因型C的高风险表型的分子基础及其临床意义 - EurekAlert!

Molecular basis and clinical significance of the high-risk phenotype of hepatitis B virus genotype C - EurekAlert!
新闻简报一项由Xia & He Publishing Inc.于2026年9月24日发表的研究探讨了乙型肝炎病毒基因型C的高风险表型,强调了其临床相关性。该研究确定基因型C是一种与延迟HBeAg血清转换、持续病毒复制、活跃的炎症反应以及更快进展至纤维化和肝硬化相关的慢性HBV感染形式。此外,与基因型B相比,该基因型与肝细胞癌(HCC)风险增加有关,其风险比(HR)约为2.35,而荟萃分析的比值比(OR)约为2.05。这种高风险特征归因于特定的病毒突变,如BCP A1762T/G1764A和preC G1896A,这些突变会影响HBeAg的表达和病毒复制。此外,免疫失调、致癌信号传导以及病毒整合也增加了HCC的风险。该研究强调,基因型C应被视为一种增强风险的病毒学因素,而不是监测或慢性乙型肝炎决策的独立决定因素。它提倡将基因型数据与其他临床和分子标志物相结合,以优化慢性乙型肝炎患者长期风险评估和管理策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
Home Article Highlight | 2026年9月24日 乙型肝炎病毒基因型C的高风险表型的分子基础及其临床意义 Xia & He Publishing Inc.
地点与对象
东亚和大洋洲;患有慢性乙型肝炎感染的患者。
事件
Xia & He Publishing Inc.发表的一项研究突出了乙型肝炎病毒基因型C的高风险表型的分子基础及其临床意义,强调其与延迟HBeAg血清转换、持续病毒复制、活跃的炎症反应、更快的纤维化进展以及更高的肝细胞癌(HCC)风险相关。
规模、影响与风险
与基因型B相比,基因型C与更高的HCC风险相关(HR≈2.35;荟萃分析OR≈2.05)。它还表现出持续的病毒复制、延迟的HBeAg血清转换,并且即使在抗病毒药物抑制后,也更有可能发展为肝硬化和HCC。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究讨论了基因型C作为增强风险的病毒学因素的临床意义。它建议将基因型信息与其他生物标志物和临床因素结合,以提高风险评估和监测规划的效果。研究结果基于分子和临床证据,未提及特定的暴发或公共卫生应对措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Molecular basis and clinical significance of the high-risk phenotype of hepatitis B virus genotype C - EurekAlert!

News Brief
A study published on 24-Sep-2026 by Xia & He Publishing Inc. explores the high-risk phenotype of hepatitis B virus genotype C, highlighting its clinical relevance. The research identifies genotype C as a form of chronic HBV infection linked to delayed HBeAg seroconversion, persistent viral replication, active inflammation, and a faster progression to fibrosis and cirrhosis. It also notes a higher risk of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) compared to genotype B, with a hazard ratio of approximately 2.35 and an odds ratio of 2.05 from meta-analysis. The high-risk profile is attributed to specific viral mutations, such as BCP A1762T/G1764A and preC G1896A, which affect HBeAg expression and replication. Additionally, immune dysregulation, oncogenic signaling, and viral integration contribute to the increased HCC risk. The study emphasizes that genotype C should be considered a risk-enhancing factor rather than a standalone determinant for treatment or surveillance decisions. It advocates for integrating genotype data with other clinical and molecular markers to refine long-term risk assessment and management strategies for patients with chronic hepatitis B.
Original source text (English)
Home Article Highlight | 24-Sep-2026 Molecular basis and clinical significance of the high-risk phenotype of hepatitis B virus genotype C Xia & He Publishing Inc. Facebook X LinkedIn WeChat Bluesky Message WhatsApp Email image: HBV genotype C represents a clinically relevant high-risk form of chronic HBV infection. It is generally associated with delayed HBeAg seroconversion, persistent viral replication, more active hepatic inflammation, faster fibrosis progression, and a higher risk of HCC. Importantly, this risk may persist even after effective antiviral suppression, especially in patients with advanced fibrosis or cirrhosis. The high-risk phenotype of genotype C appears to be related to its viral sequence background, enrichment of BCP/preC/HBx-related variants, altered HBeAg expression, immune dysregulation, HBx-related oncogenic signaling, and HBV integration-associated genomic instability. Although these mechanisms are not exclusive to genotype C, they may be more clinically relevant in genotype C infection because of the prolonged replication phase, enrichment of high-risk viral variants, and greater cumulative inflammatory and oncogenic injury. In clinical practice, genotype C should be viewed as a risk-enhancing virological factor rather than a stand-alone determinant of treatment or surveillance decisions. Combining genotype information with viral mutations, fibrosis stage, cirrhosis status, treatment response, and emerging biomarkers may help refine long-term risk assessment and surveillance planning for patients with chronic hepatitis B. view more Credit: Lei Sang, Tao Shen HBV genotype C is common in East Asia and linked to cirrhosis and HCC. It features persistent replication, delayed HBeAg seroconversion, active inflammation, and high HCC risk. Key drivers include BCP A1762T/G1764A, preC G1896A, abnormal HBx, and viral integration. This review summarises the molecular features, pathogenic mechanisms, and clinical implications, and discusses genotype‐informed risk stratification. Introduction CHB progression varies widely. Viral factors, especially genotype and mutations, are key determinants. Genotype C is associated with poorer outcomes. BCP/preC mutations produce high replication with low HBeAg expression; HBx dysfunction and viral integration contribute to inflammation and hepatocarcinogenesis. Virological Features Distribution: Predominant in Asia/Oceania. Subgenotypes: C1 (Southeast Asia), C2 (China, Korea, Japan), C3/C4 (Oceania). Mixed infection may increase replication and inflammation. Genome: ~3.2 kb circular DNA with four overlapping ORFs. In genotype C, sequence background influences emergence of regulatory mutations. PreC and BCP are critical for HBeAg expression and replication. A1762T/G1764A is more frequent in genotype C. Nucleotide 1858 (C in genotype C, T in B) affects G1896A stability. Key mutations: BCP A1762T/G1764A: Reduces HBeAg, enhances pgRNA and replication; causes HBx K130M/V131I. Associated with increased HCC risk, stronger in genotype C. preC G1896A: Stop codon, abolishes HBeAg. Constrained by ε stem‐loop; less frequent and later in genotype C. Marker of HBeAg‐negative CHB. Synergy: Co‐occurrence of BCP and preC mutations increases cirrhosis/HCC risk. Quasispecies: Viral diversity under immune pressure promotes accumulation of high‐risk variants. Molecular Mechanisms HBeAg loss and immune dysregulation: Reduced HBeAg disrupts immune tolerance, causing persistent ineffective inflammation, repeated hepatocyte injury, and fibrosis. HBx/BCP variants: Activate Wnt/β‐catenin (via APC/GSK3β, SFRP1/5 repression) and JAK/STAT3 (via ROS, mitochondrial dysfunction). K130M/V131I and truncated HBx enhance oncogenic signalling. Host disturbances: Epigenetic changes, DNA damage response defects, p53 dysfunction, autophagy abnormalities cooperate. Viral integration: Detectable in ~85–90% of HBV‐related HCC. Integration is a byproduct of DNA repair; X‐gene fragments produce truncated HBx. Recurrent genes include TERT, KMT2B/MLL4, PLEKHG4B. Integration alone is insufficient to define a driver. Clinical Consequences Natural history: Longer immune‐active phase, delayed HBeAg seroconversion, faster fibrosis/cirrhosis than genotype B. HCC risk: Independent risk factor vs. genotype B (HR ≈ 2.35; meta‐analysis OR ≈ 2.05). Antiviral therapy: Nucleos(t)ide analogues suppress replication but do not eliminate residual HCC risk, especially with cirrhosis. Integrated HBV DNA and clonal expansion persist. Biomarkers (qHBsAg, HBcrAg, HBV RNA) may help but are not standardised. Risk stratification: Genotype C is a risk modifier, not a stand‐alone marker. Combine with age, sex, family history, HBV DNA, ALT, fibrosis, cirrhosis, metabolic factors, and treatment response. Management: Six‐monthly ultrasound remains standard. Genotype C justifies strict surveillance adherence and active risk reassessment, not automatic interval shortening. It is not a single treatment indication but may support earlier reassessment in borderline cases. Perspectives Most evidence treats genotype C as one category; subgenotype C2 data are limited. Future priorities: validate genotype‐informed risk models; clarify residual risk mechanisms; test Wnt/STAT3 targeting; define role of immunotherapy; assess feasibility of routine genotyping. Conclusions Genotype C is a high‐risk form of CHB, associated with delayed HBeAg seroconversion, persistent replication, active inflammation, faster fibrosis, and higher HCC risk—persisting after viral suppression. Its phenotype relates to BCP/preC/HBx variants, immune dysregulation, oncogenic signalling, and integration‐associated genomic instability. Genotype C should be viewed as a risk‐enhancing factor, not a stand‐alone determinant. Combining genotype with viral mutations, fibrosis, cirrhosis, treatment response, and emerging biomarkers may refine long‐term risk assessment and surveillance. The Journal of Clinical and Translational Hepatology (JCTH) is owned by the Second Affiliated Hospital of Chongqing Medical University and published by XIA & HE Publishing Inc. JCTH publishes high quality, peer reviewed studies in the translational and clinical human health sciences of liver diseases. JCTH has established high standards for publication of original research, which are characterized by a study’s novelty, quality, and ethical conduct in the scientific process as well as in the communication of the research findings. Each issue includes articles by leading authorities on topics in hepatology that are germane to the most current challenges in the field. Special features include reports on the latest advances in drug development and technology that are relevant to liver diseases. Regular features of JCTH also include editorials, correspondences and invited commentaries on rapidly progressing areas in hepatology. "乙型肝炎:病毒载量高时即使肝酶水平正常也推荐积极治疗" - 体育朝鲜

新闻简报本文讨论了韩国最新的慢性乙型肝炎(慢性乙型肝炎)治疗指南,强调从依赖肝酶水平转向监测病毒载量,特别是乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)DNA水平,以决定抗病毒治疗(治疗)。这一转变基于研究显示,肝酶水平正常但病毒载量高的患者面临更高的肝损伤和肝癌风险。指南建议对病毒载量中等(2,000至108 IU/mL)或高病毒载量的患者,尤其是具有风险因素(如年龄较大或有肝癌家族史)的人群,尽早开始抗病毒治疗。重点在于长期抑制病毒,以防止进展为肝硬化、肝衰竭和肝细胞癌。患有慢性乙型肝炎的患者被建议每六个月进行一次肝癌筛查,包括超声检查和血液检测。此外,家庭成员和密切接触者应接受HBsAg和抗-HBs检测,以确定感染状态和免疫力。文章强调了持续服药的重要性,并避免突然中断抗病毒治疗。它还提到了多学科团队在管理疾病晚期阶段(如肝硬化或肝癌)中的作用。
查看新闻五要素
时间
现在重点是早期抑制病毒复制,以预防肝硬化和肝癌。" 乙型肝炎是由乙型肝炎病毒(乙型肝炎病毒)感染肝细胞引起的。
地点与对象
大多数成年人在急性感染后可自然恢复,但新生儿或婴儿期感染,或免疫功能低下者感染,可能持续超过六个月并发展为慢性乙型肝炎(慢性乙型肝炎)。
事件
推荐了一种新的慢性乙型肝炎(慢性乙型肝炎)治疗策略,将重点从肝酶水平转移到病毒载量,特别是HBV DNA水平,以确定抗病毒治疗(治疗)的适用性。
规模、影响与风险
修订后的指南强调早期抑制病毒复制以预防肝硬化和肝癌。据报道,韩国约60%的肝癌死亡病例与乙型肝炎有关,突显了早期治疗和管理的重要性。
应对、状态与不确定性
这些指南基于最近的研究和临床试验,建议对病毒载量高且有风险因素的患者进行积极抗病毒治疗(治疗)。现在,病毒载量中等的患者也被纳入抗病毒治疗(治疗)的范畴,而病毒载量低的患者则通过定期随访检查进行监测。

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Korean)
"Hepatitis B: Active treatment recommended when viral load is high even if liver enzyme levels are normal" - 스포츠조선
News Brief
The article discusses updated treatment guidelines for chronic hepatitis B in South Korea, emphasizing the shift from relying on liver enzyme levels to monitoring viral load, specifically HBV DNA levels, for treatment decisions. This change is based on research showing that patients with normal liver enzymes but high viral load face a higher risk of liver damage and cancer. The guidelines recommend early antiviral treatment for those with moderate viremia (2,000 to 108 IU/mL) and high viral load, particularly in individuals with risk factors such as older age or a family history of liver cancer. The focus is on long-term suppression of the virus to prevent progression to cirrhosis, liver failure, and hepatocellular carcinoma. Patients with chronic hepatitis B are advised to undergo regular liver cancer surveillance, including ultrasonography and blood tests, every six months. Additionally, family members and household contacts should be tested for HBsAg and anti-HBs to determine infection status and immunity. The article highlights the importance of consistent medication adherence and avoiding abrupt discontinuation of antiviral therapy. It also mentions the role of multidisciplinary care in managing advanced stages of the disease, such as cirrhosis or liver cancer.
Original source text (Korean)
Latest Celebrity K-Pop Drama Movie Culture K-Beauty Latest Celebrity K-Pop Drama Movie Culture K-Beauty Home Culture "Hepatitis B: Active treatment recommended when viral load is high even if liver enzyme levels are normal" By Jongho, Jang Published 2026-09-23 10:02 Share this article X Facebook Copy Copy URL copied. File photo courtesy of Pixabay [Sportschosun, Jang Jong-ho] For years, liver enzyme levels were one of the key criteria for determining antiviral treatment for patients with hepatitis B. However, treatment strategies are changing בעקבות research showing that even patients with normal liver enzyme levels face a higher risk of liver damage and liver cancer if they have a high hepatitis B viral load. Yoo Jeong-ju, a professor of gastroenterology at Soonchunhyang University Bucheon Hospital, said, "Recently, the treatment of chronic hepatitis B has shifted away from waiting until inflammation develops in the liver. The focus is now on suppressing viral replication early to prevent cirrhosis and liver cancer." Hepatitis B is caused by the hepatitis B virus infecting liver cells. Most adults who become acutely infected recover naturally, but infections acquired during the newborn or infant period, or while a person is immunocompromised, can persist for more than six months and develop into chronic hepatitis B. Once the disease becomes chronic, inflammation and fibrosis gradually progress in the liver and can lead to cirrhosis, liver failure and hepatocellular carcinoma. The number of people with chronic hepatitis B in South Korea is estimated at approximately 1.2 million. Although the diagnosis rate is relatively high at about 83%, only 22.2% actually receive treatment, leaving a substantial gap between diagnosis and treatment. Approximately 60% of liver cancer deaths in South Korea are reportedly attributable to hepatitis B, making early diagnosis and management crucial. Acute hepatitis B infection can cause fatigue, loss of appetite, a low-grade fever, nausea, jaundice and dark urine. Chronic hepatitis B, however, is often asymptomatic until the liver has sustained considerable damage. The most common route of infection is vertical transmission from an infected mother to her child at birth. It can also spread through blood transfusions, exposure to contaminated needles, invasive procedures, sexual contact, and exposure to blood or bodily fluids among family members. It is not transmitted through ordinary meals, hugging, sneezing or other casual contact. The 2026 revised clinical guidelines redefine chronic hepatitis B as a viral infectious disease rather than an inflammatory liver disease. They also shift the main criterion for treatment decisions from liver enzyme levels, particularly ALT, to viral load, measured by HBV DNA. As a result, moderate viremia, which was often monitored rather than actively treated when liver enzyme levels were normal, has been added as a new treatment category. Moderate viremia refers to a blood viral load ranging from 2,000 to 108 IU/mL. A recent large-scale cohort study in South Korea found that patients in this range had a higher risk of developing hepatocellular carcinoma than patients with high viremia, defined as an HBV DNA level of 108 IU/mL or higher. An interim analysis of a joint randomized clinical trial in South Korea and Taiwan also confirmed that early antiviral treatment substantially reduced the risk of serious clinical events, including liver cancer, decompensated liver disease and death. Patients with a viral load below 2,000 IU/mL, who do not face a high risk of liver damage, are monitored through regular follow-up examinations. Active antiviral treatment is recommended for patients with high viremia who have risk factors such as older age, a family history of liver cancer or liver fibrosis. The standard treatment for chronic hepatitis B is to suppress viral replication with oral antiviral medications such as tenofovir and entecavir. Patients who achieve a complete cure by eliminating the hepatitis B virus remain rare. The practical treatment goal is to suppress HBV DNA to undetectable levels over the long term, thereby preventing the progression of liver inflammation and fibrosis and reducing the risk of cirrhosis, liver failure and liver cancer. Even when the virus is well controlled with antiviral treatment, patients whose liver fibrosis or cirrhosis has already progressed remain at risk of developing liver cancer. Accordingly, patients with chronic hepatitis B should undergo liver cancer surveillance, including liver ultrasonography and blood tests, every six months. Family members and household contacts of patients with hepatitis B should undergo tests for hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) and surface antibody (anti-HBs) to determine whether they are infected and whether they have immunity. Those who are infected should undergo liver enzyme and liver fibrosis tests to assess the extent of liver damage, with liver ultrasonography or a biopsy performed when necessary. Those who are not infected but lack antibodies can receive vaccination. Patients already diagnosed with chronic hepatitis B should avoid excessive alcohol consumption and be cautious about taking dietary supplements or folk remedies whose efficacy and safety have not been established. In particular, abruptly stopping antiviral medication without a doctor's instructions can cause rapid viral reactivation and worsen hepatitis, making consistent medication adherence and regular medical appointments important. When hepatitis B progresses to cirrhosis or liver cancer, close multidisciplinary cooperation among internal medicine, surgery, radiology and radiation oncology is essential. Soonchunhyang University Bucheon Hospital has a well-established multidisciplinary care system that enables efficient coordination among departments and prompt treatment. With additional liver transplant specialists recently joining the hospital, minimally invasive procedures such as laparoscopic and robotic surgery are expected to expand, allowing the hospital to provide more patient-centered care. Professor Yoo Jeong-ju said, "Hepatitis B is a disease whose risks of cirrhosis and liver cancer can be greatly reduced through early detection and appropriate treatment and management." She emphasized, "If you have never been tested for hepatitis B, it is advisable to check whether you are infected and whether you have antibodies through a health screening or other examination. Patients with hepatitis B should also undergo regular testing so they do not miss the appropriate time to begin treatment." B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

停用乙肝药物改用草药是否安全?‘肝衰竭致死’案例中的医疗责任... - 健康朝鲜

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报This article discusses a case involving a woman in her 50s with chronic hepatitis B who stopped her antiviral medication and took herbal medicine, leading to a severe deterioration in her liver function and eventual death. The Korea Medical Dispute Mediation and Arbitration Agency reviewed the case and found that the clinic, B Korean Medicine Clinic, failed to adequately explain the risks of discontinuing antiviral treatment and the potential harm of herbal medicine. The patient’s liver enzyme levels rose significantly after stopping the medication, and she developed jaundice and hepatic encephalopathy before passing away. While the clinic was not directly found to have caused her death, the agency noted that insufficient explanations and follow-up contributed to the missed opportunity for early treatment. The case was resolved with a settlement of 20 million won in compensation to the family. The article emphasizes the importance of not stopping hepatitis B medication without medical guidance and the need for caution when using herbal medicine or supplements, as they may interact with existing conditions or treatments.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该病例报告在2009年至2020年的调解记录中有所记载,患者于2019年11月停用抗病毒药物药物,其死亡发生在2020年在多家医院持续使用治疗之后。该文章发表于2026-09-21。
地点与对象
该案例涉及一位50多岁的韩国女性,她在B韩医诊所接受治疗治疗。受影响的人群包括患有慢性乙型肝炎的患者以及使用草药或补充剂的人群。
事件
一位患有慢性乙型肝炎的女性停用了抗病毒药物药物并服用草药,导致肝功能恶化并最终死亡。韩国医疗纠纷调解与仲裁机构审查了此案,并得出结论,认为诊所未能充分履行解释和监测停药及使用草药相关风险的责任。
规模、影响与风险
该案例突显了自行停用抗病毒药物药物治疗乙肝的风险,以及使用草药可能带来的潜在并发症。患者的肝功能显著恶化,导致肝衰竭和死亡。诊所被认定未能履行监测和解释风险的责任,这可能影响了及时进行治疗治疗的时机。
应对、状态与不确定性
The Korea Medical Dispute Mediation and Arbitration Agency concluded that the clinic did not adequately explain the risks of stopping antiviral medication and the need for monitoring. However, the agency also found insufficient evidence to directly link Saenggan-tang to the patient’s death. The case was resolved through a settlement, with the clinic compensating the family 20 million won.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Korean)
Is it okay to stop hepatitis B medication to take herbal medicine? Clinic responsibility in a 'liver failure death' case... - 헬스조선
News Brief
This article discusses a case involving a woman in her 50s with chronic hepatitis B who stopped her antiviral medication and took herbal medicine, leading to a severe deterioration in her liver function and eventual death. The Korea Medical Dispute Mediation and Arbitration Agency reviewed the case and found that the clinic, B Korean Medicine Clinic, failed to adequately explain the risks of discontinuing antiviral treatment and the potential harm of herbal medicine. The patient’s liver enzyme levels rose significantly after stopping the medication, and she developed jaundice and hepatic encephalopathy before passing away. While the clinic was not directly found to have caused her death, the agency noted that insufficient explanations and follow-up contributed to the missed opportunity for early treatment. The case was resolved with a settlement of 20 million won in compensation to the family. The article emphasizes the importance of not stopping hepatitis B medication without medical guidance and the need for caution when using herbal medicine or supplements, as they may interact with existing conditions or treatments.
Original source text (Korean)
Hepatitis B is a disease in which the hepatitis B virus, HBV, causes inflammation of the liver. If it becomes chronic, it can lead to cirrhosis or liver cancer, so patients should, when necessary, manage the condition by taking antiviral medication consistently. In particular, stopping antiviral medication on one’s own during treatment can allow the virus to replicate again and worsen hepatitis, so caution is required. In severe cases, it can progress to liver failure with a rapid decline in liver function. Health Chosun summarized a case from the Korea Medical Dispute Mediation and Arbitration Agency’s mediation records in which a patient being treated for hepatitis B stopped antiviral medication and took herbal medicine, after which her liver function deteriorated and she died. ◇Case overview A, a woman in her 50s, had been taking antiviral medication for chronic hepatitis B since around 2009. From May 2019 she took the antiviral drug Viread tablets, and in August of the same year she received an in-person consultation at B Korean Medicine Clinic and was prescribed Saenggan-tang. For about four months thereafter, she received additional prescriptions for Saenggan-tang via telephone consultations. In November 2019 A stopped taking Viread. Afterwards her liver enzyme levels rose and she experienced indigestion, nausea, and fatigue. In December of that year, the indicators used to assess liver cell damage, AST, GOT, rose to 240 IU/L and ALT, GPT, rose to 495 IU/L. During a January 2020 telephone consultation, medical staff at B Korean Medicine Clinic, having noted this condition, advised A to seek hospital care and to be admitted to a higher-level hospital. However, about five days later A’s ALT rose to 1000 IU/L and jaundice appeared, and she was admitted to another hospital. Despite receiving antiviral treatment, about a week later she developed hepatic encephalopathy with decreased consciousness. She was transferred to another hospital to consider the possibility of a liver transplant, but liver function did not recover, and after continued treatment at several hospitals she ultimately died. ◇Family, “Due to herbal medicine and stopping medication” vs Clinic, “No evidence Saenggan-tang caused side effects” The family argued that the herbal medicine’s toxicity and the discontinuation of antiviral medication contributed to A’s rapid deterioration. They also said that although A complained of abnormal symptoms, appropriate measures were not taken and the opportunity for timely treatment was missed. By contrast, B Korean Medicine Clinic denied there was any basis to conclude that Saenggan-tang caused A’s rapid deterioration and death. ◇"Causality not confirmed... explanations and monitoring were insufficient" The Mediation Agency judged that it was difficult to view the mere prescription of Saenggan-tang to A as inappropriate, and that a clear causal relationship between Saenggan-tang and acute-on-chronic liver failure was not established. Because B Korean Medicine Clinic knew that A planned to stop the antiviral medication she had been taking for a long time, the agency said the clinic should have sufficiently explained the possibility of viral reactivation and hepatitis worsening after stopping the medication, the need for ongoing testing, and similar risks. It also found that although A reported indigestion, nausea, and fatigue and her liver enzyme levels rose significantly after stopping the antiviral medication, the clinic’s follow-up observation was inadequate, for example by not recommending an in-person examination or additional testing. However, the agency found insufficient evidence that B Korean Medicine Clinic directly encouraged the discontinuation of the antiviral medication, and it was also difficult to conclude that the inadequate explanations and follow-up observation directly caused A’s liver failure and death. The agency noted that A herself did not immediately accept the recommendation for hospitalization, which also contributed to the missed opportunity for appropriate treatment. In the end, the Mediation Agency concluded that B Korean Medicine Clinic did not adequately fulfill its duty to explain and to monitor progress, thereby infringing on A’s right to choose treatment and opportunity for early treatment, and proposed a settlement awarding the bereaved family 20 million won in compensation. Both parties accepted this proposal and the mediation was concluded. ◇Do not stop hepatitis B medication on your own... be cautious with herbal medicine and supplements Patients with chronic hepatitis B should not stop antiviral medication on their own. Antiviral drugs suppress viral replication to reduce liver damage, and stopping them can allow the virus to increase again and worsen hepatitis. Decisions about stopping medication should be made with medical staff based on liver condition, viral load, and treatment response, and after stopping medication the virus and liver enzyme levels should be regularly monitored. When taking herbal medicine or dietary supplements, it is also advisable to inform medical staff that you have liver disease and to tell them what medications you are currently taking. Not all herbal medicines or supplements are harmful to the liver, but some ingredients can cause liver damage, and products that combine multiple ingredients can make it difficult to identify the cause if problems arise. If you experience unusually severe fatigue, loss of appetite, persistent nausea, yellowing of the skin and the whites of the eyes indicating jaundice, or darkening of the urine, you should seek medical care as these may indicate worsening liver function. Health Chosun summarized a case from the Korea Medical Dispute Mediation and Arbitration Agency’s mediation records in which a patient being treated for hepatitis B stopped antiviral medication and took herbal medicine, after which her liver function deteriorated and she died. ◇Case overview A, a woman in her 50s, had been taking antiviral medication for chronic hepatitis B since around 2009. From May 2019 she took the antiviral drug Viread tablets, and in August of the same year she received an in-person consultation at B Korean Medicine Clinic and was prescribed Saenggan-tang. For about four months thereafter, she received additional prescriptions for Saenggan-tang via telephone consultations. In November 2019 A stopped taking Viread. Afterwards her liver enzyme levels rose and she experienced indigestion, nausea, and fatigue. In December of that year, the indicators used to assess liver cell damage, AST, GOT, rose to 240 IU/L and ALT, GPT, rose to 495 IU/L. During a January 2020 telephone consultation, medical staff at B Korean Medicine Clinic, having noted this condition, advised A to seek hospital care and to be admitted to a higher-level hospital. However, about five days later A’s ALT rose to 1000 IU/L and jaundice appeared, and she was admitted to another hospital. Despite receiving antiviral treatment, about a week later she developed hepatic encephalopathy with decreased consciousness. She was transferred to another hospital to consider the possibility of a liver transplant, but liver function did not recover, and after continued treatment at several hospitals she ultimately died. ◇Family, “Due to herbal medicine and stopping medication” vs Clinic, “No evidence Saenggan-tang caused side effects” The family argued that the herbal medicine’s toxicity and the discontinuation of antiviral medication contributed to A’s rapid deterioration. They also said that although A complained of abnormal symptoms, appropriate measures were not taken and the opportunity for timely treatment was missed. By contrast, B Korean Medicine Clinic denied there was any basis to conclude that Saenggan-tang caused A’s rapid deterioration and death. ◇"Causality not confirmed... explanations and monitoring were insufficient" The Mediation Agency judged that it was difficult to view the mere prescription of Saenggan-tang to A as inappropriate, and that a clear causal relationship between Saenggan-tang and acute-on-chronic liver failure was not established. Because B Korean Medicine Clinic knew that A planned to stop the antiviral medication she had been taking for a long time, the agency said the clinic should have sufficiently explained the possibility of viral reactivation and hepatitis worsening after stopping the medication, the need for ongoing testing, and similar risks. It also found that although A reported indigestion, nausea, and fatigue and her liver enzyme levels rose significantly after stopping the antiviral medication, the clinic’s follow-up observation was inadequate, for example by not recommending an in-person examination or additional testing. However, the agency found insufficient evidence that B Korean Medicine Clinic directly encouraged the discontinuation of the antiviral medication, and it was also difficult to conclude that the inadequate explanations and follow-up observation directly caused A’s liver failure and death. The agency noted that A herself did not immediately accept the recommendation for hospitalization, which also contributed to the missed opportunity for appropriate treatment. In the end, the Mediation Agency concluded that B Korean Medicine Clinic did not adequately fulfill its duty to explain and to monitor progress, thereby infringing on A’s right to choose treatment and opportunity for early treatment, and proposed a settlement awarding the bereaved family 20 million won in compensation. Both parties accepted this proposal and the mediation was concluded. ◇Do not stop hepatitis B medication on your own... be cautious with herbal medicine and supplements Patients with chronic hepatitis B should not stop antiviral medication on their own. Antiviral drugs suppress viral replication to reduce liver damage, and stopping them can allow the virus to increase again and worsen hepatitis. Decisions about stopping medication should be made with medical staff based on liver condition, viral load, and treatment response, and after stopping medication the virus and liver enzyme levels should be regularly monitored. When taking herbal medicine or dietary supplements, it is also advisable to inform medical staff that you have liver disease and to tell them what medications you are currently taking. Not all herbal medicines or supplements are harmful to the liver, but some ingredients can cause liver damage, and products that combine multiple ingredients can make it difficult to identify the cause if problems arise. If you experience unusually severe fatigue, loss of appetite, persistent nausea, yellowing of the skin and the whites of the eyes indicating jaundice, or darkening of the urine, you should seek medical care as these may indicate worsening liver function
